  │ as chat turns │ becomes a user turn and an assistant turn before the    │
  │               │ real request.                                           │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │               │ Answer only with words copied exactly from TARGET       │
  │ S5, reminder  │ VERSE. right before Answer:. Copying a history          │
  │               │ rendering that isn't in the verse is the 1.7B's most    │
  │               │ common failure.                                         │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ S6, field     │ GATEWAY PHRASE first, then TARGET VERSE, the history    │

  │               │ are reordered to match.                                 │
  └───────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

      637  ### Step 5. No history (LIVE, DEV-NOHIST)

⏺ I wrote the Step 4 results into the plan, and none of S1–S6 is adopted. So the
  starting point for the next steps (S*) stays X4b. Below, DEV is compared with
  X4b and the no-history set with the baseline. "Confident wrong" means wrong
  at confidence 80 or more.

  ┌────────────┬────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
  │            │ 1.7B DEV   │     1.7B      │                               │
  │  Variant   │   top-1    │  no-history   │            Verdict            │
  │            │            │     top-1     │                               │
  ├────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │            │ 44.6 →     │               │ No. 4B is worse (p 0.01),     │
  │ S1 short   │ 44.1% (p   │ 16.4 → 19.3%  │ 0.6B's confident wrong        │
  │            │ 0.37)      │ (p 0.02)      │ answers double, and it is no  │
  │            │            │               │ faster                        │
  ├────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ S2         │ 44.6 →     │ 16.4 → 13.5%  │                               │
  │ procedure  │ 43.3% (p   │ (p 0.003)     │ No. Worse on both sets        │
  │            │ 0.003)     │               │                               │
  ├────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │            │ 44.6 →     │               │                               │

  │            │ 7e-7)      │               │                               │

  │ S4 chat    │ 44.6 →     │ 16.4 → 23.2%  │ Closest, but fails the        │
  │ turns      │ 45.1% (p   │ (p 2e-7)      │ adoption rule                 │
  │            │ 0.44)      │               │                               │
  ├────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ S5         │ 44.6 →     │ 16.4 → 13.3%  │                               │
  │ reminder   │ 43.1% (p   │ (p 0.006)     │ No                            │
  │            │ 0.009)     │               │                               │
  ├────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ S6 field   │ 44.6 →     │ 16.4 → 9.9%   │ No. Without history the 1.7B  │
  │ order      │ 44.5% (p   │ (p 6e-6)      │ gives up twice as often       │
  │            │ 1.0)       │               │                               │
  └────────────┴────────────┴───────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

  S4 fails the rule for two reasons:
  - The 1.7B's gain on DEV isn't significant.
  - The 4B is significantly worse on DEV: 51.7 → 49.4%, p 0.002.

  S4 still has the most going for it of the six:
  - Fewer confident wrong answers on every model. On DEV, 1.7B 492 → 278, 0.6B
    343 → 6, 4B 529 → 437. This matters because the UI applies any suggestion
    that has a confidence.
  - 0.6B gains on DEV. 39.5 → 41.5%, p 0.004.
  - The 1.7B is faster. Median time drops from 165 to 128 ms.

  Part of that drop comes from lower confidences overall: the 1.7B's mean
  confidence on right answers falls from 86 to 80. It seems to copy the lower
  confidences in the examples.

  Where S4 loses cases:
  - Requests with no possible answers listed: mostly answers of "",0. That's 14
    of 15 lost cases for the 1.7B and 22 of 26 for the 4B.
  - Requests that list possible answers: the 1.7B gains (25 cases won, 12 lost),
    but the 4B loses (28 won, 51 lost).

  S1 doesn't help the stock models. llama-server caches the system prompt, so
  the shorter prompt isn't faster. It stays a retrain candidate only to test
  whether a fine-tune needs the long prompt.

  My recommendation is to take S4, and S1 if you still want the speed test, into
  the Step 7 retrains. They would be retrain candidates, not changes to the
  prompt that ships. A fine-tune learns the format from its training data, so
  the 4B's drop on the stock model matters less there. Before an S4 retrain, I
  need to check how mlx-lm masks multi-turn examples, so training only learns
  from the last answer.

  Two other options:
  - Try S4 with the separate no-history prompt (N3). That would target S4's
    empty answers on requests without possible answers.
  - Run Step 6 next.

  Nothing is committed.

⏺ I've started N3 + N4 (I named it N34) on all three models, on DEV and the
  no-history set. Step 4 had already finished, so there was nothing to wait for.
  It should take about 25 minutes.

  What N34 sends:
  - With history: the same prompt as X4b, the current best.
  - With no history: N3's separate prompt, which never mentions previous
    translations and caps confidence at 70. It adds X3's gateway verse line,
    which shows the verse in the gateway language with the phrase in [brackets].
    The request text is the same as N4's.
  - Examples: each of the prompt's three examples now has a gateway verse line
    too. For the Swahili one I wrote "Jesus healed many of [the sick]".

  Tests: I added two for N34, and all 61 variant tests and lint pass.

  When the runs finish, I'll compare N34 with the baseline on the no-history
  set, which is where it should help. I'll also check that DEV hardly changes.
  Most DEV requests have history, so they get the same prompt as X4b. Then I'll
  record the results in the plan.

✻ Baked for 48s · done Monday 4:47 AM

※ recap: We're improving the single-request prompt for small models, and Steps 4 and 5 are now done with nothing adopted beyond the confidence cap. Next, you decide whether to run Step 6 (released fine-tunes) or start the S4 retrain.

❯ what is step 6                                                                

⏺ Step 6 measures what changing the app's prompt would cost people already using
  the released fine-tuned models, before any retraining. Those models were
  trained on the app's current prompt, so a new one might make them worse.

  What it runs: the app's current prompt (baseline) and the final candidate (F).
  Each runs on the two released fine-tunes:
  - uw-tw-qwen3-1.7b-d3, GGUF Q5_K_M
  - the 0.6B companion, GGUF Q6_K

  Both are scored on TEST (the fine-tune's held-out files) and TEST-NOHIST (the
  same cases with the history removed).

  What it shows:
  - How much F would cost users of the released models right away.
  - How the fine-tunes do with no history at all. They were never trained on
    that.

  When it's done: four rows per fine-tune in the plan's results section (2
  prompts × 2 sets).

  What F would be today: the only prompt change that passed the adoption rule is
  X4b, which adds the algorithm's possible answers to the request. Everything
  in Steps 4 and 5 failed, so F = X4b. The no-history cap (N5) is a change in
  the app's code, not the prompt, so it applies to both runs.

  That makes Step 6 a baseline-vs-X4b comparison on the fine-tunes. That's about
  4 runs, roughly 30–40 minutes with both models.

✻ Brewed for 8s · done Monday 5:12 AM

❯ summarize what options we have tried for the prompt and what were the results 

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Of the 19 prompt variants measured, one was adopted: X4b, which adds the
  algorithm's possible answers. One code change was also adopted, the N5
  confidence cap. Everything was scored on stock Qwen3 GGUF models run through
  llama-server.

  - Primary model: the stock 1.7B.
  - DEV: 1,500 cases the app sends to the AI.
  - No-history set: 750 of those cases with the history removed.
  - Adoption rule: the 1.7B must gain top-1 significantly (p < 0.05) on one set
    without getting worse on the other. The 0.6B and 4B must not get
    significantly worse.

  The 1.7B scores 41.0% on DEV and 16.4% on the no-history set with the app's
  current prompt.

  Step 3: extra information in the request

  What the results show

  - Only information the model can't work out itself helped the 1.7B, mainly the
    algorithm's candidates (X4b). Rewording the rules (S1, S2, S5, S6) did
    nothing or made it worse.
  - Without history, the stock 1.7B stays near 16–19% whatever we try, except
    S4's examples as chat turns (23%). The 4B gains from the gateway verse, the
    translationWords term and the separate prompt. Those didn't pass because the
    decision rests on the stock 1.7B.
  - Confident wrong answers matter, since the app shows any suggestion that has 
    a confidence. N5 removes them with no history, and S4 nearly halves them
    with history.
  - The 0.6B is near 1% without history whatever the prompt.
  - Candidates for the retrains (Step 7): X4b, S4, and N4 or N3+N4 for
    no-history examples. S1 would be included only to test whether a fine-tune
    needs the long prompt. A fine-tune can learn to use these where the stock
    models couldn't.

  The full tables are in plans/improve-single-prompt.md.

✻ Crunched for 26s · done Monday 5:14 AM

❯ are there any changes to the prompt itself that may improve the results for   
  a qwen3 model                                                                 

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes. A few changes look worth measuring, but none has been tested yet, so
  treat these as ideas. Each one targets a failure we saw in the results.

  1. Let the server limit answers to verse words. llama-server accepts a grammar
  field with each request, and we can build one per request that only allows
  quoted runs of TARGET VERSE words. The 1.7B's most common mistake is copying a
  previous translation that isn't in the verse, and the rule against it isn't
  followed: with X1, it still copied a rendering 223 times out of 422 when none
  was in the verse. A grammar makes that impossible.
  - plans/improve-llm-suggestions.md (D1) rejected grammars because answers
    never failed to parse. This is a different use, about content rather than
    format.
  - Cost: per-request code (the fixed llmExtraParams can't carry it), and only
    llama.cpp-based servers support it.
  - Test: an X4b + grammar variant on DEV.

  2. Base confidence on the model's token probabilities. The model's stated
  confidence barely tells right answers from wrong ones. With X4b, the 1.7B's
  mean confidence is 86 on right answers and 83 on wrong ones. That leaves 492
  confident wrong answers, and the app shows any suggestion that has a
  confidence.
  - llama-server can return the probability of each output token (n_probs). The
    probability of the answer's first token usually separates right from wrong
    much better than a stated number.
  - The prompt could then stop asking for a confidence at all. That would also
    remove the examples' confidences, which S4 suggested the model copies.
  - Test: rescore saved answers offline first. That would show whether it helps
    before any new runs.

  3. Have the model choose among the possible answers by letter. X4b already
  lists the algorithm's candidates, and the 1.7B picks the first one about 92%
  of the time. Labeling them A/B/C with a "D: other verse words" option is a
  format small models handle well. It would also give a cleaner probability for
  idea 2.
  - Risk: it could tie the model even more closely to the algorithm.

  4. Use Qwen3's recommended sampling settings. For non-thinking mode, Qwen
  recommends temperature 0.7, top_p 0.8 and top_k 20. We use 0.1, the app's
  default, and these other settings weren't tested here.
  - Test: scripts/test-llm-temperatures.sh plus LLM_EVAL_EXTRA_PARAMS for top_p
    and top_k. It's cheap.
  - Caution: an earlier temperature sweep is why 0.1 is the default, so the gain
    may be small.

  5. Measure thinking mode on a small budget. Thinking is always off
  (/no_think). For a 1.7B, thinking often helps on matching tasks like this one,
  at a large cost in speed.
  - Test: one run with a capped max_tokens would show whether a smaller thinking
    model can beat the 4B. If not, it can be ruled out.

  Not worth repeating: rewording the rules (S1, S2, S5, S6), extra examples
  (N2), or merging the system prompt into the user message (S3). All made the
  1.7B worse or no better.

  The goal is a better fine-tune, and a fine-tune learns the format and rules
  from its training data. Ideas 1 and 2 work outside the prompt, so they would
  help the fine-tunes as well as the stock models. I'd test those two first,
  starting with 2 because it needs no new model runs.

⏺ Both tests are running in the background, about 5 hours in total: Qwen's
  recommended settings first, then thinking. I'll report when they finish.

  - Test 4, Qwen's settings (about 35 min): X4b with Qwen's recommended
    non-thinking settings, temperature 0.7, top_p 0.8 and top_k 20. It runs on
    all three models, DEV and no-history, so the adoption rule can be checked.
  - Test 5, thinking (about 4.5 h): X4b with thinking on, using Qwen's thinking
    settings (0.6, 0.95, 20) and answers capped at 2,048 tokens. It runs on the
    1.7B only, on both sets. If it helps the 1.7B, I'll run the 0.6B and 4B
    afterwards.

  Results from a 30-case thinking trial:
  - Speed: about 7 seconds per request, against about 0.17 s now, roughly 40
    times slower.
  - Length: the 1.7B thinks for 636 tokens at the median and over 1,700 for the
    slowest tenth.
  - Accuracy: 17 of 30 right, against 18 for X4b. That's too few cases to tell.

  Even if thinking helps a lot, 7 seconds a check may be too slow for the app.
  That's for you to judge once the numbers are in.

  The app always sends thinking off, so test 5 needed a small change:
  - getBestSelections: a new llmEnableThinking option, off by default. Nothing
    in the app sets it.
  - The runner: an LLM_TRAINING_THINKING=1 setting to turn it on.
  - A test: checks that thinking is off unless the option is set, and that
    /no_think is left out when it is on. It passes and lint is clean.

  Nothing is committed.

⏺ Qwen's recommended sampling settings changed nothing, but thinking gave the
  biggest gain of anything we've tried, on both sets. The catch is speed:
  thinking takes about 6 seconds per check instead of 0.17 seconds.

  Qwen's recommended sampling (0.7 / 0.8 / 20), all three models:

  ┌───────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  │ Model │       DEV top-1       │ No-history top-1 │
  ├───────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 1.7B  │ 44.6 → 44.3% (p 0.34) │ 16.4 → 16.1%     │
  ├───────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 0.6B  │ 39.5 → 38.7% (p 0.17) │ 1.1 → 1.5%       │
  ├───────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 4B    │ 51.7 → 51.9%          │ 34.0 → 34.0%     │
  └───────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────┘

  Confident wrong answers and times didn't change either. The app's temperature
  of 0.1 stays.

  Thinking on the 1.7B, compared with X4b:

  ┌──────────┬─────────────────┬───────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
  │          │      Top-1      │ Cases gained  │ Median time (slowest 10% │
  │          │                 │    / lost     │        take over)        │
  ├──────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ DEV      │ 44.6 → 50.3% (p │ 136 / 50      │ 165 ms → 6.1 s (15 s)    │
  │          │  2e-10)         │               │                          │
  ├──────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ No       │ 16.4 → 36.1% (p │ 184 / 36      │ 123 ms → 9.4 s (17 s)    │
  │ history  │  4e-25)         │               │                          │
  └──────────┴─────────────────┴───────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

  - With thinking, the 1.7B is level with the 4B without thinking on DEV: 50.3%
    against 51.7%, no significant difference.
  - With no history it more than doubles, and beats the 4B on the app's prompt
    (34.0%). It doesn't beat the 4B with N3 + N4 (42.9%).
  - It thinks for 847 tokens at the median.
  - 10% of requests (221) hit the 2,048-token cap. Those almost certainly ended
    without an answer and fell back to the algorithm, so a higher cap might gain
    more, but would be slower.

  The decision for you is about speed, not the prompt. At 6–9 seconds a check,
  thinking only makes sense if suggestions are filled in the background before
  the translator reaches a check, not while they wait. The app's queue already
  works through a whole group in the background, so it might fit there. The
  fine-tunes were trained without thinking, so they would need training examples
  that include it.

  Possible next runs, none started:
  - The 0.6B with thinking, about 2 hours. It would be the faster option if it
    gains as much.
  - The 4B with thinking, about 7 hours.
  - The 1.7B with a 4,096-token cap, to see what the cut-off answers would have
    given.

  Recorded in the plan: both results, as a new Step 5A.

  Code change: an experiment-only llmEnableThinking option in getBestSelections,
  off in the app, plus LLM_TRAINING_THINKING in the runner. There's a test for
  it, and lint is clean.

  Nothing is committed.

      802  ### Step 6. Cost to the released fine-tunes (LIVE, TEST)
      803 

⏺ I agree. At 6–9 seconds a check, and up to 20 seconds on the slowest, a whole
  group would take too long even running in the background. I recorded the
  decision in the plan's Step 5A, and I won't run the 0.6B or 4B with thinking.

  The run still shows something useful: the same 1.7B reaches 4B-level accuracy
  when it works the answer out. So what limits it is reasoning, not knowledge of
  the languages. That points to two directions that don't cost time when the
  app asks:

  1. Teach the fine-tune what thinking found (no thinking in the app). The
     fine-tune already learns from translators' real choices, so thinking can't
     give it better answers to learn. It could add training examples, though.
     Thinking can label the many history-less verses that have no translator
     answer yet. Using those would mean also trusting answers that are wrong
     about half the time, so it's a risk. I'd only try it with a confidence
     filter, and I can't promise a gain.
  2. Do the reasoning in code instead of the model. X4b's possible answers
     already work this way: the algorithm does part of the matching, and the
     1.7B gained 3.6 points. The no-history case is where thinking gained most
     (16 → 36%). There the code could give the model what it would otherwise
     reason out, for example the gateway verse. That helped the 4B, but not the
     1.7B without a retrain.

  I'd move on to Step 6, measuring baseline against X4b on the released
  fine-tunes. Then Step 7: retraining with X4b, S4 and the no-history handling.

  The experiment-only llmEnableThinking option is still in getBestSelections,
  off by default. I'd keep it for later experiments, but it's a few lines to
  remove if you'd rather not have an unused option in src. Nothing is committed.

⏺ Step 7 is ready and queued: R0 starts training as soon as Step 6 finishes,
  then R1. I'll add the R2 and R3 variants once Step 6 is done, and report Step
  6's results then.

  Exporter changes (no src changes):
  - Training examples can be built with any prompt variant
    (LLM_FINETUNE_PROMPT_VARIANT). For X4b they include the algorithm's full
    option list.
  - A share of examples can be added again with no history
    (LLM_FINETUNE_NO_HISTORY_SHARE), labelled 55, the existing confidence for a
    guess.
  - glVerse is added to the training cases, for the gateway-verse variants. The

⏺ Step 6 is done, and Step 7 hasn't started. In short, the released fine-tunes
  shouldn't get X4b without a retrain.

  ┌─────────────┬────────────┬───────────────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │  Fine-tune  │    Set     │  App's prompt → X4b   │ Confident wrong (≥80) │
  ├─────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ 1.7B Q5_K_M │ TEST       │ 67.4 → 66.7% (p 0.51) │ 99 → 141              │
  ├─────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ 1.7B Q5_K_M │ No history │ 54.3%, identical      │ 0 (capped)            │
  ├─────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ 0.6B Q6_K   │ TEST       │ 64.0 → 63.5% (p 0.62) │ 96 → 154              │
  ├─────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ 0.6B Q6_K   │ No history │ 36.2%, identical      │ 0 (capped)            │
  └─────────────┴────────────┴───────────────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  - Switching to X4b now would hurt users of the released models. Top-1 doesn't
    change significantly, but confident wrong answers rise 42% on the 1.7B and
    60% on the 0.6B. The fine-tunes were never trained on the possible-answers
    list, and when one of those answers is wrong they copy it with high
    confidence anyway.
  - No-history runs match exactly under both prompts. Without history the
    algorithm has no candidates, so X4b sends the same request as the app.
  - The fine-tunes handle no history far better than the stock models. The 1.7B
    gets 54.3% against the stock 1.7B's 16.4%, and the 0.6B gets 36.2% against
    1.1%. These are different case sets, so the comparison is only indicative.
    They were never trained on empty history, though their training data
    includes cases with unrelated history, which is close. Their confidence
    stays around 56–61 for right and wrong answers alike, close to the 55 they
    were trained to give for a guess.

  What this means for Step 7:
  - The prompt change only makes sense together with a retrain, which is what R1
    tests.
  - The retrains will show whether training on no-history examples (R0) and the
    possible answers (R1) improves on these numbers.

  I recorded the results in the plan under Step 6. Step 7's data, configs and
  script are ready, but nothing will start until you say so.

※ recap: We're improving the AI prompt for the small Qwen3 models; Step 6 showed the released fine-tunes shouldn't get the new prompt without a retrain. Step 7's retrain data and configs are ready, waiting for your go-ahead to start training. (turn off recaps in /config)

❯ what is x4b?  is that the only recommendation now                             

⏺ X4b adds the algorithm's candidate answers to the request. Before asking the
  AI, the app already runs its own matching algorithm. When that finds
  candidates, X4b lists them in a POSSIBLE ANSWERS block, best first:

  PREVIOUS TRANSLATIONS:
  "vương quốc của Thiên Chúa" (chosen 16 times for "kingdom of God")
  ...

  POSSIBLE ANSWERS:
  "vương quốc của Thiên Chúa"
  "vương quốc"

  GATEWAY PHRASE (en): kingdom of God

  Those requests also get a slightly changed system prompt:
  - it explains the block ("may be missing, and may be wrong");
  - it adds one rule: "POSSIBLE ANSWERS are often right: check them first, but
    answer with other verse words when none of them means GATEWAY PHRASE";
  - the first example shows the block.

  When the algorithm finds nothing (always the case with no history), the
  request is exactly the app's current prompt. The "b" marks that difference
  from X4, which used the changed system prompt for every request and hurt the
  4B on no-history requests.

  Is it the only recommendation? No, and it shouldn't ship now. Here's where
  everything stands:

  Change: N5: cap AI confidence at 70 when there's no history (code, not
    prompt)
  For stock models: Confident wrong answers with no history fall to 0
  For the released fine-tunes: Hardly matters (they answer around 56–61 there)
  Recommendation: Ship. It's in src, uncommitted
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: X4b: possible answers in the request
  For stock models: 1.7B top-1 41.0 → 44.6% (p 8e-5)
  For the released fine-tunes: Top-1 unchanged, but confident wrong answers
    +42% (1.7B) and +60% (0.6B)
  Recommendation: Don't ship yet. Only with a fine-tune retrained on it (R1
    tests that)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: N34, S4, S1
  For stock models: Didn't pass the adoption rule on the stock 1.7B
  For the released fine-tunes: Not tested
  Recommendation: Retrain candidates only (R1–R3)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Change: Thinking
  For stock models: Big gain (1.7B 50.3%)
  For the released fine-tunes: —
  Recommendation: Rejected: too slow

  Most users will run the fine-tuned models, which are better than any
  stock-model prompt: the 1.7B fine-tune is at 67% on TEST. So for now, the only
  change worth shipping is the N5 confidence cap. Any prompt change depends on
  Step 7 showing that a fine-tune retrained on it beats the R0 control.

      910  On the stock 1.7B, 0.6B and 4B: `baseline` and `F` on TEST and

⏺ Neither retrain clearly beats the released model (D3), so neither passes the
  adoption rule. R0 is the better of the two, and R1 has a specific weakness.

  R0 (the app's prompt, plus 10% of examples copied with no history):
  - TEST: 67.4 → 69.1% (p 0.12).
  - No history: 54.3 → 56.0% (p 0.13).
  - Confident wrong answers: 99 → 77 (−22%).
  - Empty answers with no history: 34 → 9.
  - Speed: the same.

  Each gain is small and not significant on its own, but it's better everywhere
  and costs nothing.

  R1 (trained on N34, the prompt with possible answers and the no-history
  gateway verse):
  - TEST: level with R0 (69.2%).
  - Possible answers: they no longer cause confident wrong answers (82, against
    141 for the released model given X4b), so training fixes the problem Step 6
    found.
  - No history: it's significantly worse than R0 (52.7 vs 56.0%, p 0.026).

  That drop all comes from one project, en_ult_tit, the only one with English as
  the target language. R1 answered "" on 123 of its 291 no-history cases and
  fell to 29.9%, against 42.6% for R0. On every other project, R1 equals or
  beats R0.

  The training data has no English-target examples at all, since that project is
  held out for testing. I checked that the gateway phrase was marked correctly
  in all of those verses. The likely explanation, which I haven't proved, is
  that R1 never saw an English verse next to a gateway verse in another
  language.

  My recommendation:
  1. R0's no-history training data is worth keeping. It's the one Step 7 change
     with no downside.
  2. The next run should be R1b, X4b only. That means possible answers with
     history, and the app's plain prompt with none. It keeps what R1 does with
     history and avoids the no-history problem. One more run, about 3.5 hours.
  3. R2 and R3 (short prompt, chat-turn examples) are less useful now. Both
     build on R1, so I'd decide on them after R1b.

  Caveats:
  - TEST is being used to choose between runs. With so few runs that risk is
    small, and it's noted in the plan.
  - English-target projects are probably rare among real users, but the R1
    failure would hit them hard.

  The results are recorded in the plan under Step 7. Nothing else is running,
  and nothing is committed.

❯ what was the n5 cap                                                           

⏺ The N5 cap limits the AI's confidence to 70 when a check has no previous
  translations (no selection history from other projects). It's a code change,
  not a prompt change.

  Why it was added:
  - How the app uses confidence: tc-ui-toolkit applies any AI suggestion that
    has a confidence, with no threshold.
  - The measurement: without history, the stock models' answers were right only
    about 20–28% of the time (1.7B) and 22–57% (4B), whatever confidence they
    gave.
  - The problem: they still often said 80–100. On the no-history set that was
    273 confident wrong answers for the 1.7B, 78 for the 0.6B and 367 for the
    4B.

  What it does (src/utils/checkingSuggestionsUtils.js):

  export const LLM_NO_HISTORY_MAX_CONFIDENCE = 70;

  export function capConfidenceWithoutHistory(result, previousTranslationData) {
    const hasHistory = Object.values(previousTranslationData ||
  {}).some(renderings => Object.keys(renderings || {}).length);
    if (hasHistory || !result?.bestSelections?.length) return result;
    return { ...result, bestSelections: result.bestSelections.map(option => ({
  ...option, confidence: Math.min(option.confidence,
  LLM_NO_HISTORY_MAX_CONFIDENCE) })) };
  }

  getBestSelections applies it to each AI result. Requests with any history are
  untouched, and the answers themselves don't change, only their confidence.

  What it did in the tests:
  - Confident wrong answers on the no-history set fell to 0 for all three stock
    models, with the answers unchanged.
  - For the fine-tunes it hardly matters: without history they already answer
    around 55–61, the confidence they were trained to give for a guess.

  Choosing the number: 70 was the plan's default. Any cap below 80 gives the
  same numbers, because 80 is where "confident" is counted. A cap of 50 would
  match the measured accuracy better. It's one constant to change if you prefer
  it.

  Status: it's in src with three tests, uncommitted. It works with any model, so
  it's the one change from this work that could ship now.

✻ Worked for 39s · done Tuesday 6:54 AM

※ recap: We're improving the small Qwen3 suggestion models, and retraining on extra no-history examples (R0) beat both released models, with everything now committed. Next is your call on releasing R0 as the new 1.7B and 0.6B models.

❯ whats next                                                                    

⏺ The next step is deciding whether to release the R0-trained models. The prompt
  work is finished: the app's prompt stays, the N5 cap is committed, and Step 9
  ("put the result in the app") has nothing to do.

  1. Decide whether to release R0. This is your call, and it's what I'd do next.
  - The 0.6B is a clear case. It gains 5.4 points with no history (p 0.0002) and
    isn't significantly worse with history.
  - The 1.7B is consistent but modest. It's ahead in every run, with about 20%
    fewer confident wrong answers and 8 more points on the suite. No single run
    is significant.

  If you go ahead, the release work is:
  - MLX and GGUF builds, through llmTraining/make_release.sh;
  - updated model cards with the new numbers;
  - a short hand-check of some no-history answers (llmTraining/handcheck.py).

  That's about an hour plus your review.

  2. Things that could still improve the models:
  - English-target training data. No project in the training data has English as
    the target. That's why R1 failed there, and why every model is weakest on
    English targets (about 40% with no history, against 63% elsewhere). A second
    English-target project, kept out of the test set, would fix the gap.
  - Calibrating confidence from token probabilities. Stated confidence still
    separates right from wrong answers poorly. I suggested earlier testing
    confidence from the probabilities the model gives its own answer tokens. It
    can be checked offline first, with no new runs.
  - A higher share of no-history examples. 10% helped. 20% might help more,
    especially for the 0.6B. That's one retrain each.

  3. Small cleanups:
  - Ignore llmTrainingData/finetune-* in .gitignore.
  - Decide whether to keep or remove the experiment-only llmEnableThinking
    option.
  - Update CLAUDE.md's fine-tune line (68% held-out, D3) if R0 is released.

  I'd start with the release decision, then try confidence calibration, since it
  needs no new model runs.

  I ran the algorithm on each and refreshed the unhelpful-case check.

  A problem I found and fixed for the Bengali file: the first build gave English
  GL phrases, all 286 of them. The cause is the resource itself. The Arabic
  BSOJ arst v4.9 bible has English text (UST-like) aligned to the Greek in 51 of
  its books. Only a few books, Titus and 1 Timothy among them, are in Arabic.
  avd v6.9 is Arabic throughout.

  So I added LLM_TRAINING_GL_BIBLE to the builder, which names the GL bible to
  use, and rebuilt the file from avd. It now has Arabic phrases, and lint
  passes.

  The same problem is in two older training files:
  - vi_glt_luk_book_BSOJ_ar: 2,582 of 2,584 "Arabic" GL phrases are English.
  - ru_rlob_luk_book_BSOJ_ar: all 1,274 are English.

  So most of the Arabic-GL training data in every model so far, including the
  released D3 and R0, was really English. My suggestion is to rebuild both from
  avd. It's two commands, and git checkout restores the old files if needed. I
  haven't done it yet because it changes data the earlier runs used, so retrains
  from now on wouldn't be comparable with them. Tell me and I'll rebuild them.

  This probably affects translationCore users too: the app takes a check's GL
  phrase from the first Arabic bible that has aligned text, which is arst. If
  so, an Arabic-GL user checking Luke, Galatians, 2 Corinthians and most other
  books would see English GL phrases. That's worth reporting to whoever
  maintains the BSOJ resources. Of the existing files, only
  en_ult_tit_book_BSOJ_ar (Titus) has real Arabic phrases from arst, and that's
  a test file.

  None of the new data files, the builder changes or the LLM_TRAINING_GL_BIBLE
  setting are committed.

  released model (D3) and R0, and the gains are significant. It's the first
  retrain to pass Step 7's adoption rule.

  ┌─────────────┬───────┬──────────┬─────────────┬──────────────┬─────────┐
  │    Model    │ TEST  │   No     │ Confident   │  Empty (no   │ Median  │
  │             │       │ history  │    wrong    │   history)   │   ms    │
  ├─────────────┼───────┼──────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼─────────┤
  │ D3          │ 67.4% │ 54.3%    │ 99          │ 34           │ 77      │
  │ (released)  │       │          │             │              │         │
  ├─────────────┼───────┼──────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼─────────┤
  │ R0          │ 69.1% │ 56.0%    │ 77          │ 9            │ 80      │
  ├─────────────┼───────┼──────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼─────────┤
  │ R0v2        │ 72.3% │ 65.0%    │ 83          │ 1            │ 80      │
  └─────────────┴───────┴──────────┴─────────────┴──────────────┴─────────┘

  - Against D3: +4.9 points on TEST (p 2e-5) and +10.7 with no history (p
    9e-14).
  - Against R0: +3.2 on TEST (p 0.007) and +9.0 with no history (p 1e-9).
  - Speed: the same.

  Where the gain comes from: mostly the English-target test files (en_ult_tit),
  which improve with every gateway language. With no history:

  ┌────────────┬───────────┬────────┐
  │     GL     │ R0 → R0v2 │   p    │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ Indonesian │ 38 → 69%  │ 0.0001 │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ Russian    │ 52 → 74%  │ 0.002  │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ Arabic     │ 29 → 45%  │ 0.02   │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ Spanish    │ 52 → 65%  │ 0.02   │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ Persian    │ 38 → 56%  │ 0.05   │
  └────────────┴───────────┴────────┘

  The other test projects hold level with history and improve slightly without
  it (Bengali Ephesians 70 → 81%).

  Caveat: the TEST files with the biggest gains share their language pairs with
  the new training projects (English target with Arabic or Russian GL), though
  not their books. So part of the gain may be that pairing, and the improvement
  for language pairs not in the training data could be smaller.

  Before a release, I'd do two things:
  1. Confirm without a seed, as in Step 8: two runs plus the suitability suite,
     about 15 minutes.
  2. Train the 0.6B on the same data, about 2.5 hours.

  Then the release itself: builds through make_release.sh, updated model cards,
  and a hand-check.

  The results are in the plan as Step 8A. Nothing is running, and nothing is
  committed: the plan and lora-r16-r0v2.yaml.

⏺ Both finished, and the new training data makes both models clearly better than
  the released ones.

  R0v2 (1.7B) confirmed without a seed. Both runs match the seeded result:

  ┌───────────────────┬────────────────────┬────────┐
  │                   │ Released D3 → R0v2 │   p    │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ TEST, run a       │ 67.4 → 72.5%       │ 1e-5   │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ TEST, run b       │ 67.8 → 71.9%       │ 0.0004 │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ No history, run a │ 53.5 → 65.0%       │ 1e-15  │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ No history, run b │ 53.8 → 64.7%       │ 1e-15  │
  └───────────────────┴────────────────────┴────────┘

  Confident wrong answers fell from about 98 to 80, and speed is the same
  (median 80 ms).

  One mixed signal: on the suitability suite, R0v2 scored 64%, between D3 (62%)
  and R0 (70%).
  - The suite is small: about 3 cases per run separate R0 from R0v2.
  - Its real cases come from projects that are also in the training data.

  So I'd trust TEST, which is held out by whole project, but the drop is noted
  in the plan.

  The 0.6B on the new data:

  ┌────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┬───────┐
  │                            │ Released 0.6B → new 0.6B │   p   │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────┤
  │ TEST                       │ 64.0 → 67.7%             │ 0.002 │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────┤
  │ No history                 │ 36.2 → 49.7%             │ 6e-16 │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────┤
  │ Empty answers (no history) │ 104 → 9                  │       │
  └────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴───────┘

  It's significantly better on both sets, at the same speed (48 ms median). With
  history, the new 0.6B now matches the released 1.7B (67.7% against 67.4%).

  Recommendation: release both as the next versions (R0v2 1.7B and R0v2 0.6B).
  The app's prompt doesn't change, so translationCore needs nothing new.

  Remaining release work:
  - builds through llmTraining/make_release.sh (MLX and GGUF);
  - model cards with the new numbers and training projects;
  - a short hand-check of some no-history answers;
  - updating CLAUDE.md's fine-tune line.

  llmTraining/outputs/uw-tw-qwen3-1.7b-r0v2-GGUF/ and

⏺ The 4B is training on the same data and settings as the new 1.7B (R0v2), using
  Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 as the base. It will take about 13 hours, and I'll
  report when it's done.

  - Progress: the loss is falling normally, from 2.14 to 0.27 by step 40.
  - Speed and memory: about 10 seconds per step (the 1.7B took about 4), using
    about 8 GB of memory.
  - Time: 4,345 steps plus regular validation come to roughly 12–13 hours.
    Conversion to GGUF Q5_K_M and scoring on TEST and TEST-NOHIST follow
    automatically.

  The machine will be busy until then. translationCore's own llama-server would
  slow down the training if it's used heavily in the meantime.

  Setup:
  - Base model: Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507, the same 4B used in every stock
    comparison. It has no thinking mode. I checked that R0v2's data renders
    correctly with its template, and that only the answer is trained on.
  - Config: llmTraining/lora-r16-r0v2-4b.yaml, which differs from the 1.7B's
    only in the base model and output folder.
  - Script: run-retrain.sh now takes the base model name, so it works for this
    model.

  When it finishes, I'll compare it with the new 1.7B (72.3% with history, 65.0%
  without) and the stock 4B (58.6% and 36.0%). The question is whether 4B's
  size gains enough to justify about twice the size and time per suggestion. If
  it does, I'll also make its MLX build and a model card.

  Logs go to llm-eval-results/step7/r0v2-4b/. Nothing is committed.

  │ Qwen3-4B-2507     │          │         │            │         │ GB   │
  └───────────────────┴──────────┴─────────┴────────────┴─────────┴──────┘

  - Against the new 1.7B:
    - With history, +1.4 points, not significant (p 0.29; 50 cases gained, 39
      lost).
    - With no history, +4.2 points, significant (p 0.006).
    - It takes twice as long per suggestion (157 against 80 ms), with a file 2.4
      times larger.
  - Against the stock 4B: fine-tuning lifts it 15 points with history and 33
    without, and cuts confident wrong answers by two-thirds.

  My take: the 1.7B is still the right default. It's as accurate when history is
  available, which is the usual case once a project is underway, at half the
  cost. The 4B's real advantage is a project's first checks, with no history
  yet. It could be released as a third, "large" option for computers with memory
  and speed to spare. That would mean an MLX build, a model card and a
  release-script entry. I haven't done those, since you didn't ask for a
  release.

  Done:
  - The results are in the plan under Step 8A, and the GGUF is in
    llmTraining/outputs/uw-tw-qwen3-4b-r0v2-GGUF/.
  - run-retrain.sh now handles base models whose weights are split across
    several files, and can resume after training (SKIP_TRAIN=1).

  Not committed: the plan, lora-r16-r0v2-4b.yaml, both model cards and
  make_release.sh.

  twice as slow. If you want it as a third, "large" option, I'll make its MLX
  build, add a card, and add it to make_release.sh.

  3. Two bugs found along the way:
  - App: for the history, fetchPreviousSelectionData always uses the first GL
    bible, even when that book isn't aligned in it. Arabic-GL users get no
    GL-keyed history for most books. A small fix in checkingSuggestionsUtils.js,
    and I can make it.

    Arabic-GL users likely see English GL phrases in translationCore. That's for
    whoever maintains BSOJ's resources.

  should be updated once the release is decided.

  card TODOs.

          ingData` through `getBestSelections` and saves results and summ

          TrainingData.test.js`). Without `LLM_TRAINING_URL` it uses the

          e app's fallback to the algorithm, and prints the top-1 at each
           possible `ALGORITHM_CERTAIN_CONFIDENCE` (saved as `results/<mo

          cted word in the history, exactly or as another form by `wordSi
          milarity`, or an answer unusually long for its language: over t
          hat language's 99th percentile from `getAnswerLengthLimits` and
           over the longest history rendering + 2 words; languages space
          words differently, so there is no fixed word limit) and saves t

          s__/helpers/suggestionsHelpers.js`; the unit tests in `__tests_
          _/findUnhelpfulTrainingCases.test.js` run in `npm test`, the sc
          an does not.

      26  - `llmTraining/` — the Python side of that fine-tune (Apple MLX
          , `mlx-lm`; its own `.venv`, git-ignored, as are `outputs/`, `.
          venv-gguf/` and `.llama.cpp/`). `llmTraining/README.md` has set
          up, training, fusing and the GGUF conversion for Windows and Li
          nux. `check_data.py` checks the exported data renders like the
          app's prompt (run it after `build:finetune-data`); the `*.yaml`
           files are the training runs of the plan (`lora-r16-d3.yaml` is
           the chosen one: LoRA r16 on the base set plus the `noRelatedWo
          rdInHistory` cases, built with `LLM_FINETUNE_INCLUDE=noRelatedW
          ordInHistory LLM_FINETUNE_OUT_DIR=llmTrainingData/finetune-d3`)
          ; `train_full.py` runs a full fine-tune in float32; `handcheck.
          py` makes a local page for hand-checking a sample of a case gro
          up. The resulting model, `uw-tw-qwen3-1.7b-d3`, scores 68% top-
          1 on the held-out files against 52% for stock `qwen3-1.7b` and
          59% for `qwen3-4b-2507` (results at the end of the plan). Relea
          se builds (`llmTraining/make_release.sh`, cards in `llmTraining
          /model-card*/`): MLX 5-bit for Mac, GGUF Q5_K_M for Windows and
           Linux; a small companion on Qwen3-0.6B (`lora-r16-d3-0.6b.yaml
          `, 64-65% held-out top-1, fails the suite's top-1 bar) as MLX 6
          -bit and GGUF Q6_K, for computers that cannot run the 1.7B. Sco
          re GGUF builds through plain llama.cpp (`llama-server`): LM Stu
          dio's llama.cpp engine on the Mac gives lower, different answer
          s for Qwen3-1.7B (stock too). The fine-tune is trained on singl
          e requests only: it must be used with one request per query (`l
          lmGrouping` 1, the default), since with batches it drops the `#
          ## REQUEST n` markers and most answers come back empty. Changin
          g the system prompts or `formatPreviousTranslations` means rebu
          ilding the data and retraining.

      59  - No `llmQueryUrl` → `getBestTWordSelectionWithConfidenceAlgori
          thm(wordList, targetLangCode, glPhrase, glLangCode, previousTra
          nslationData)` for each job. This path is deterministic and mak

          se (exact key, else weighted word overlap with short GL words d

          poolRenderings` merges the renderings of all keys. Each pooled
          rendering is searched in the verse: contiguous, then in order w
          ith gaps (polynomial tightest placement), then a tight out-of-o
          rder window, then the rendering words the verse contains. If no
           candidate has a confidence of `FUZZY_PASS_BELOW_CONFIDENCE` (5
          0) or more, an order-aware fuzzy pass matches inflected or comp

          ndering`), which only chooses between candidates for the same w
          ords and breaks ties (`plans/algorithm-partial-match.md` holds
          its rationale). The reported confidence, which also orders the
          options, is `scoreSuggestionFromHistory`: closeness to the hist
          ory rendering (verbatim 1, lower for gaps, swapped or missing w
          ords and fuzzy forms) x GL-key weight (1 for the exact key, 0.3
          6-0.6 for related keys) x verse checks (the words occur more th
          an once in the verse, a word of an exact-key rendering is next

  GGUFs on TEST and TEST-NOHIST with the current prompt, under a new results

⏺ The test that checks the baseline variant byte for byte against the app's
  prompt passes, so these runs send exactly what the app now sends. Starting
  them, about 20 minutes:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The tests on the three new models (1.7B, 0.6B, 4B) are running, about 20
  minutes. I'll report when they finish.

  What the merge changed (commit c074e87):
  - Single requests with no history now get a separate system prompt, the N3
    one. It doesn't mention previous translations, asks for a meaning match, and
    caps confidence at 70.
  - Requests with history are unchanged, byte for byte. The other prompt change
    in the merge, markOtherKey, is off by default.

  So only the no-history requests send something new to the models, and they
  were trained with the old prompt there. The commit message says the released
  D3 fine-tunes weren't significantly worse with it, but these new models were
  trained with 10% no-history examples on the old prompt, so they might react
  differently.

  The runs: each model gets TEST and TEST-NOHIST with the app's current prompt,
  at the same settings as before. The runs have their own names (…-appn3), so
  the earlier results stay. Each is compared case by case with the same model on
  the old prompt:
  - TEST should come out identical, apart from any test case whose history
    produces no lines.
  - TEST-NOHIST is the real test.

  If the new prompt costs the fine-tunes accuracy, the choices are to build the
  training data with the new prompt and retrain, or to keep the old prompt for
  fine-tuned models.

⏺ The merged prompt change doesn't affect the new fine-tuned models: none of the
  three changes significantly.

  ┌───────┬───────────────┬────────────────────┬────────────────────┬──────┐
  │       │  TEST (with   │ TEST-NOHIST, old   │   Answers that     │      │
  │ Model │   history)    │    prompt → new    │  changed (lost /   │  p   │
  │       │               │                    │      gained)       │      │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 1.7B  │ 72.3%,        │ 65.0 → 65.0%       │ 16 / 16            │ 1.0  │
  │       │ identical     │                    │                    │      │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 0.6B  │ 67.7%,        │ 49.7 → 50.2%       │ 16 / 20            │ 0.62 │
  │       │ identical     │                    │                    │      │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 4B    │ 73.7%,        │ 69.2 → 68.7%       │ 14 / 10            │ 0.54 │
  │       │ identical     │                    │                    │      │
  └───────┴───────────────┴────────────────────┴────────────────────┴──────┘

  - With history, every answer is the same as before, since those requests are
    byte-identical.
  - Without history, the new prompt changes 2–4% of the answers, in both
    directions, with no net effect. Speed and empty answers are the same or
    slightly better: the 0.6B's empty answers fall from 9 to 6 and the 4B's from
    2 to 0.

  The fine-tunes learned the task itself, so the wording of the no-history
  prompt barely matters to them. The commit's note found the same for the
  released D3 models.

  What this means:
  - The new release builds (1.7B, 0.6B, 4B) can be used with the merged prompt
    as they are. No retrain is needed.
  - They were trained with the old prompt on their no-history examples. A future
    retrain will use the new prompt automatically, since the exporter builds
    examples with the app's own prompt functions, so training and use will match
    again.

  The result is recorded in the plan under Step 8A. It isn't committed, along
  with CLAUDE.md, the three model cards, make_release.sh and the 4B config.

  Where more test data could come from:

  1. Arabic, with no retrain needed. ar_avd_jer_book (Jeremiah, 1,364 aligned
  verses) isn't in the training data, so it can become a test project as it is.
  It adds Arabic as a target and also Old Testament material: TEST has only Ruth
  now. Its GL is English.

  2. More non-English GLs, with no retrain needed. Existing test projects can be
  built with other GLs. You switch the GL in translationCore as before, and the
  files land in TEST automatically.
  - bn_glt_eph (98 checks) with Russian, Arabic or Hindi; Ephesians is aligned
    in rlob, avd and Hindi glt.
  - es-419_est_rut (84) with Russian, Arabic or Hindi.
  - ne_glt_2co (323) with Arabic (avd) or Hindi.
  - Hindi would also test a GL the models have never seen.

  Use avd for any Arabic GL, since arst has English text in most books.

  3. Spanish target. es-419_ult_est_book (Esther, 167 verses) isn't in training.
  Caveat: another Spanish Esther (es-419_est_est) is, so the two share books
  and key terms.

  4. Russian, Vietnamese, Telugu, Farsi and Indonesian targets. Every aligned
  project in these languages is already training data. The only options are:
  - Hold one project per language out of training and retrain. For example
    vi_glt_heb (303 verses), fa_glt_jas, id_glt_1ti, plus a Russian and a Telugu
    book. The first release's held-out copy did this. It costs training data
    and a retrain.
  - New aligned books from those language teams.

  My suggestion: start with 1 and 2, which need no retraining. Jeremiah alone
  would add several hundred Arabic checks.

  (__tests__/helpers/promptEvalCases.js). So after building the new files I'd
  add them there and rebuild the TEST sets; DEV stays the same. Tell me which
  ones you want, and for GL switches, switch them in translationCore first.

⏺ I added Arabic Luke (ar_avd_luk_book) as a test project. TEST is now 2,166
  checks, up from 835, and it's the first test data with Arabic as the target.

  The data file: llmTrainingData/ar_avd_luk_book_unfoldingWord_en.json has 1,331
  checks from 168 groups, with English GL phrases from ULT. The project was
  already set to the English GL, and all of Luke is aligned. The algorithm gets
  66% top-1 on it.

  Making it test data:
  - Never trained on: ar_avd_luk is in the exporter's test projects
    (DEFAULT_TEST_PROJECTS), so future fine-tune builds leave it out of
    training. It was never in any earlier training data.
  - In the TEST sets: it's in FINETUNE_TEST_FILES, and its cases are appended to
    test.json and test-nohist.json.
  - Nothing else changed: I added a testadd option to the set builder
    (LLM_PROMPT_EVAL_SETS=testadd) that only appends new test files. DEV and the
    earlier 835 TEST checks are unchanged, so all earlier results stay
    comparable on them. A backup of the old TEST files is in
    llm-eval-results/promptEval-backup-20261125/.
  - Algorithm: rerun on both TEST sets.
  - Tests: all affected tests pass, and lint is clean.

  Caveat: the Arabic-GL Luke training files take their GL phrases from the same
  Arabic AVD text. So the models have seen these Arabic words for these key
  terms in their input, though never as answers, and Arabic Luke will score
  higher than an unseen Arabic book would. It's noted in the plan.

  Next: none of the models has been scored on the new checks yet, since the
  earlier results only cover the original 835. Rescoring the released and new
  fine-tunes takes about 10 minutes per model, and would give their first
  Arabic-target numbers. Shall I run it?

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
⏺ Five of the nine training languages still have no test data at all: Russian,
  Vietnamese, Telugu, Farsi and Indonesian. Together they make up 64% of the
  training examples.

  ┌────────────┬─────────────┬───────────┬─────────────────────┐
  │   Target   │ Test checks │ ± margin* │  Training examples  │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Arabic     │ 1,331       │ ±2.5 pts  │ 2,639               │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Nepali     │ 362         │ ±4.7      │ 0 (unseen language) │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ English    │ 291         │ ±5.3      │ 3,021               │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Bengali    │ 98          │ ±9.1      │ 444                 │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Spanish    │ 84          │ ±9.8      │ 134                 │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Russian    │ 0           │           │ 4,152               │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Vietnamese │ 0           │           │ 3,834               │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Telugu     │ 0           │           │ 2,340               │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Farsi      │ 0           │           │ 703                 │
  ├────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Indonesian │ 0           │           │ 113                 │
  └────────────┴─────────────┴───────────┴─────────────────────┘

  *Rough 95% margin of error on a top-1 score near 70%.

  Also thin:
  - Spanish and Bengali targets (84 and 98 checks).
  - Every non-English GL: Arabic 55, Indonesian 52, Spanish 105, Farsi 52 and
    Russian 66 checks, against 1,836 for English.

  How to get more:

  Russian, Vietnamese, Telugu, Farsi and Indonesian targets. Every aligned
  project you have in these languages is already training data, so there are
  only two routes:
  - Hold one project per language out of training and retrain. For example:
    - Vietnamese: vi_glt_heb (303 verses);
    - Farsi: fa_glt_jas or fa_glt_1ti;
    - Indonesian: id_glt_1ti;
    - Russian: ru_rlob_tit, or ru_rlob_gen for a large one;
    - Telugu: te_glt_tit.

    It costs some training data and one retrain of each model, and makes the
    TEST numbers comparable across all main languages. The first release did
    this with a held-out copy.
  - New aligned books from those language teams. That's the best test data, but
    it depends on their work.

  More non-English GLs, with no retrain needed. You switch the GL in
  translationCore, and the files go straight into TEST.
  - ar_avd_luk with the Russian GL. Luke is aligned in rlob. This is the
    easiest: about 1,300 checks with Arabic target and Russian GL.
  - bn_glt_eph or es-419_est_rut with Russian, Arabic (avd) or Hindi.
  - ne_glt_2co with Arabic (avd) or Hindi.

  Hindi would be a GL the models have never seen.

  More Spanish targets: es-419_ult_est (167 verses), not in training. The caveat
  is that another Spanish Esther is in training.

  My suggestion:
  1. Add ar_avd_luk with the Russian GL now. It needs only a GL switch.
  2. Then decide whether to hold out one project per language and retrain.
     That's the only way to measure Russian, Vietnamese, Telugu, Farsi and
     Indonesian at all.

  Gaps:
  - Targets: Spanish and Indonesian are thin on both sides, and Nepali is
    test-only on purpose.
  - GLs:
    - English and Russian are well covered.
    - Arabic has 1,748 training examples but only 639 test checks.
    - Spanish has little of either.
    - Farsi and Indonesian GLs have no training data and only 52 test checks
      each, all from English Titus.
  - Pairings without test data: Russian and Vietnamese are tested only with the
    English GL, though both train with three or four GLs. Telugu and Bengali
    have no Russian-GL test.
  - Russian GL in training: 2,784 of its 6,598 examples are English targets
    (Luke and Joshua).
